The contribution of an employee organization or professional organization can be part of this free space. A bicycle plan can be part of this free space."
    ],
    [
      "Death of Employee: If the employee dies, the employer pays the salary plus the holiday allowance up to the moment the employee died, and a death benefit. This benefit is equal to the salary the employee would receive from the day after death up to and including the last day of that month, plus two months. This amount is paid to the spouse, or if not married, to the relationship partner. If the employee was married but the spouse died, or was divorced, the amount goes to legitimate children, foster children, or biological children if they are under 18 years old. If none of the above situations apply, but there is another person with whom the employee had a relationship and for whom the employee was the breadwinner, this person receives the amounts. If this person is also not present, the employer can decide that someone else receives the amounts. If the employee had a legally mandatory disability insurance, and the surviving relatives receive a death benefit from this insurance, the death benefit based on the disability benefit is deducted from the death benefit received from the employer."

    [
      "Year-end Bonus: The employee receives a year-end bonus every year. The amount of the bonus is 8.3% of the salary the employee received in that calendar year, increased by the holiday allowance the employee accrued in that year. The employee receives the year-end bonus in December. If the employee no longer works for the employer in December, the employee receives the accrued bonus up to the moment their employment contract ended. This bonus is received with the final settlement."

The employee receives the holiday allowance every year by 31 May at the latest, covering the preceding period between 1 June and 31 May. The employer must make this decision before the date the employee would receive the periodic increase. The employer must also inform the employee in writing in advance, stating the reason why the employee will not receive the periodic increase."
    ],
    [

The employee can never receive more than two periodic increases above the maximum of their salary scale. The employer must inform the employee in writing in advance that they will receive an extra periodic increase, stating the reason why."

The employee does not receive an allowance if they observe the function because their colleague is on vacation. If the employee observes the function together with one or more colleagues, the employee receives a portion of the allowance, depending on the work they observe. The employee concludes the traineeship with a 'Proeve van Bekwaamheid' or an EVC procedure, using the assessment criteria described in the industry standard 'Advanced professional'.   "pension_information": [
    [
      "Mandatory Registration: The employer is obliged to register the employee as a participant with the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn."
    ],
    [
      "Premium Deduction: The employer may deduct a portion of the pension premium from the employee's salary. This concerns the salary as described in Article 1.10 of the Pensioenreglement of the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn."
    ],
    [
      "Employee Contribution 2017: The part that the employee pays themselves is in 2017: 11.2% of the salary for the old-age pension and partner pension. The AOW-franchise is first deducted from the employee's salary. If the employee works part-time, the AOW-franchise is deducted from the salary pro-rata to the part-time factor. The AOW-franchise is the part of the salary that the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn takes into account and over which the employee does not accrue old-age pension. 0% of the salary for the premium for the disability pension scheme, after the AP-franchise has been deducted from the salary. The AP-franchise is the part of the salary that the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn takes into account and over which the employee does not accrue disability pension with the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn."
    ],
    [
      "New Premiums: At the end of December each year, the new premiums for the following year are calculated and determined by the Overleg Arbeidsvoorwaarden Jeugdzorg. These new premiums are immediately announced on the website www.fcbjeugdzorg.nl."
    ],
    [
      "Premium Burden Changes: The change in the pension premium burden is equally divided between employer and employee. If the pension premium burden increases, the employer and the employee each pay half of the increase. If the pension premium burden decreases, the employer and the employee each pay half less."
    ],
    [
      "Pension Regulations Access: The employer ensures that the pension regulations are available to all employees on the internet. The employee can find the regulations on http://www.pfzw.nl."
    ],
    [
      "Voluntary Pension Continuation (Wachtgeldregeling 1): If the employee is entitled to 'wachtgeld', and chooses a voluntary continuation of participation in the pension scheme they had with their old employer, and continues this for at least half, then the employee is entitled to an allowance for their costs. This allowance is half of the employer's share that the employer would have paid for the employee during a full month if they had still been employed. This is based on the employer's share in the month before the employee was dismissed. If the premium percentage for the pension changed in this month, the employer pays the new percentage."
    ],
    [
      "Voluntary Pension Continuation (Wachtgeldregeling 2): If the employee is entitled to 'wachtgeld', and chooses a voluntary continuation of participation in the pension scheme they had with their old employer, and continues this for at least half, then the employee is entitled to an allowance for their costs. This allowance is half of the employer's share that the employer would have paid for the employee during a full month if they had still been employed. This is based on the employer's share in the month before the employee was dismissed. If the premium percentage for the pension changed in this month, the employer pays the new percentage."
    ],
    [
      "Pension during Long-Term Special Leave: If the employee goes on special leave for longer than two months and their salary is not paid during that period, the employee is no longer insured for salary continuation in case of incapacity for work after the second month. The employee can take out voluntary insurance for this. The employee pays the premium for this insurance themselves. The employer can decide to grant an allowance for the premiums due for the disability insurance described in Article 9.6.d.2, the other employee insurances, and the pension provision of the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn."
    ],
    [
      "Pension during Parental Leave: During parental leave, the employee is entitled to full accrual and scope of pension and 'wachtgeld'. The employee pays their share of the pension premium themselves."
    ],
    [
      "Pension during Long-Term Care Leave: During long-term care leave, the employee is entitled to full accrual and scope of pension and 'wachtgeld'. The employee pays their share of the pension premium themselves."
    ],
    [
      "Pension during Sick Leave: If the employee continues to pay their pension premiums in full during the second year of incapacity for work, the employer also pays their share of the pension premiums. The pension basis remains at the salary the employee received in the first year of their illness."

  "leave_information": [
    [
      "Full-time Work Week: According to this CAO, the employee works full-time if they work an average of 36 hours per week. The employee can also opt for a work week of a maximum of 40 hours, if the employer agrees. Terms of employment related to the length of the work week are proportionally increased. The employee may revert to this choice every year and return to a 36-hour work week."
    ],
    [
      "Work-Related Activities as Working Time: Holding consultations, participating in meetings directly resulting from work, and mandatory retraining count as working time."
    ],
    [
      "Statutory Vacation Hours (Wettelijk Verlof): If the employee works an average of 36 hours per week, they are entitled to 144 statutory vacation hours each year. If the employee works more or fewer hours, they also receive more or fewer vacation hours. The number of vacation hours is calculated pro-rata to the number of hours the employee works according to their employment contract. When the employee takes statutory vacation hours, their salary is paid as usual (in accordance with Article 7:639 BW). From 1 January 2016, this salary also includes the employee's average irregular service allowance. This allowance is calculated over the 3 months prior to the employee's first continuous vacation in the calendar year."
    ],
    [
      "Pro-rata Vacation Hours: If the employee is not employed for the entire year, they only receive vacation hours for the period they were employed."
    ],
    [
      "Carrying Over Vacation Hours: The intention is for the employee to use as many vacation hours as possible in the same year. In addition, the employee can agree with the employer to carry over the hours to the next year."
    ],
    [
      "Mandatory Vacation: The employee is obliged to take at least two consecutive weeks of vacation each year. The employer must agree with the employee in good time when they will take vacation and when they will return. If the employee and the employer have agreed on the vacation three months in advance, the employee is guaranteed to be able to take vacation during that period."
    ],
    [
      "Right to Three Consecutive Weeks of Vacation: The employee is entitled to three consecutive weeks of vacation."
    ],
    [
      "Sick During Vacation: If the employee falls ill during their scheduled vacation and can sufficiently prove this to the employer, the hours the employee is sick do not count as vacation hours."
    ],
    [
      "Vacation Accrual During Disability: If the employee is wholly or partially incapacitated for work, they accrue full vacation hours."
    ],
    [
      "Vacation Usage During Disability: If the employee is wholly or partially incapacitated for work and takes vacation hours during their illness, these hours are fully deducted from their total number of vacation hours."
    ],
    [
      "Leave Budget (Bovenwettelijk Verlof): If the employee works an average of 36 hours per week, they are entitled to 56 hours of extra leave (leave budget) each year. If the employee takes these hours, their salary is paid as usual (in accordance with Article 7:639 BW). If the employee works more or fewer hours than 36 hours per week, they also receive more or fewer leave hours. The number of hours the employee receives is calculated pro-rata to the number of hours the employee works according to their employment contract."
    ],
    [
      "Total Leave Entitlement: Together with the 144 vacation hours from Article 9.1, the employee therefore has 200 hours of leave if they work 36 hours. This is their leave balance. The total number of hours of this leave balance on an annual basis is rounded up to whole hours, in favor of the employee. If the employee is not employed for the entire year, they receive a portion of the total leave budget, pro-rata to the number of days the employee is employed. The total number of hours of this leave budget is rounded up to whole hours on an annual basis, in favor of the employee."
    ],
    [
      "Usage Options for Extra Leave Hours: The employee can use the 56 extra leave hours for the following: for free time, during which their salary is paid; the employee can have the hours paid out (per hour, the employee receives the hourly salary applicable at that moment, but no holiday allowance, year-end bonus, other allowances, or reimbursements are received for these paid-out hours); the hours can be deposited into the 'levensloopregeling' (this option only exists for existing participants in the 'levensloopregeling' who use the transitional arrangement); the employee can use the hours for costs in the context of (re)registration; the employee can use the hours for a purpose determined in consultation with the employer in the next year. The employee can make other agreements with the employer about the extra leave hours, in favor of the employee. The employee is bound by tax rules and other laws for all choices."
    ],
    [
      "Leave Accrual During Disability (Leave Budget): If the employee is wholly or partially incapacitated for work, they accrue full leave hours. This happens according to applicable legislation."
    ],
    [
      "Leave Usage During Disability (Leave Budget): If the employee is wholly or partially incapacitated for work and takes leave hours during their illness, these hours are fully deducted from the employee's leave budget."
    ],

      "Vitality Budget from 1 January 2015: The employee is entitled to a vitality budget. This right begins 10 years before the date on which the employee reaches the AOW-gerechtigde leeftijd. The amount of the vitality budget for a 36-hour work week is 79 times their hourly wage on an annual basis."
    ],

      "Pro-rata Vitality Budget: The employee who reaches the AOW-gerechtigde leeftijd during the calendar year, or is employed for a portion of the calendar year, receives this budget pro-rata. If the employee works more or less than 36 hours per week, the employee is entitled to a budget in that calendar year that is determined pro-rata to the number of hours they work. The budget is rounded up to whole hours, in favor of the employee."

      "Paid Parental Leave: If the employee is entitled to parental leave under Chapter 6 of the Wet Arbeid en Zorg, the employee can claim a budget for parental leave allowance according to the agreements in this chapter. The employee must, deviating from Article 6.5.1 of the Wet Arbeid en Zorg, apply for parental leave in writing at least three months in advance. The employee then makes agreements with the employer about: when the leave begins and ends, how many leave hours the employee takes per week, when the employee takes the leave hours during the week. The employer refers the employee to the brochure 'Toelichting op de nieuwe afspraken over ouderschapsverlof in de cao Jeugdzorg', available on www.fcbjeugdzorg.nl."

    [
      "Parental Leave Budget Calculation: The budget is calculated before the employee goes on parental leave. The calculation is as follows: The number of hours of paid parental leave is determined by the average working hours per week. This number of hours is multiplied by 13. For this total number of hours, 50% of the employee's salary minus 50% of the statutory minimum wage is paid as an allowance. Note: The reduction by 50% of the minimum wage does not apply if the employee goes on leave on or after 1 January 2015. In that case, the agreements from lid d of this article apply due to the abolition of the parental leave tax credit by the Tax Authorities."
    ],
    [
      "Parental Leave Transitional Arrangement from 1 January 2015: Due to the abolition of the parental leave tax credit by the Tax Authorities, a transitional arrangement applies from 1 January 2015. If the employee took parental leave in 2014 and this leave continues into 2015, the employee is no longer eligible for the parental leave tax credit from the Tax Authorities, and this amount is paid by the employer. If the employee goes on leave on or after 1 January 2015, but no later than 31 December 2015, the employee is no longer eligible for the parental leave tax credit from the Tax Authorities, and this amount is paid by the employer, even if the leave continues into 2016. The calculation of this budget for parental leave is then as follows: The number of hours of paid parental leave is determined by the average working hours per week. This number of hours is multiplied by 13. For this total number of hours, the employee receives an allowance of 50% of their salary. If the employee goes on parental leave on or after 1 January 2016, the employee receives 50% of their hourly salary minus 50% of the statutory minimum hourly wage for the hours they take leave according to the calculation in lid d of this article."
    ],
    [
      "Parental Leave Repayment: If the employee terminates their employment contract during their parental leave or within 6 months after a period of leave for which the employee used a parental leave budget, or if the employee has indicated that they wish to work fewer hours per week, the employer can reclaim the allowance paid during that leave period from the employee. If the employee resigns because the employer did not want to adjust the hours in the employment contract to the hours the employee worked during this period of parental leave, the employee does not have to repay the allowance that the employer paid during that leave period."

      "Long-Term Care Leave (Langdurig Zorgverlof): The employee can receive partially paid long-term care leave if they must care for one or more of the following persons because they are seriously ill: spouse, parents, step-parents, foster parents, parents-in-law, children, step-children, foster children, or in-law children. The employee can receive unpaid long-term care leave if they must care for one or more of the following persons because they are seriously ill: grandparents, grandchildren, brothers and sisters (second-degree relatives); others who are part of their household; acquaintances with whom the employee maintains a social relationship."
    ],

    [

    ],

      "Maximum Hours for Long-Term Care Leave: The maximum number of hours of long-term care leave the employee may take is determined per instance. The calculation is as follows: The number of hours of paid long-term care leave is determined by the average working hours per week. This number of hours is multiplied by 13."
    ],
    [
      "Allowance for Long-Term Care Leave: 50% of the employee's salary is paid for the leave hours actually taken."

      "Sick Leave (Arbeidsongeschiktheid): This chapter does not apply from 1 January 2016 to employees who have reached the AOW-gerechtigde leeftijd. For these employees, the statutory rules from the 'Werken na de AOW-gerechtigde leeftijd' law apply."
    ],
    [
      "Salary Basis for Disability: If the employee becomes wholly or partially incapacitated for work, the salary used is the salary the employee earns at the moment they can no longer or only partially perform their work due to illness, pregnancy, or childbirth. This salary is adjusted if the monthly salary changes due to the agreements in Article 7 or if the monthly salary changes due to the agreements on salary increases for this CAO. Other wage components are also included in the salary (according to Article 16, first paragraph, of the Wet financiering sociale verzekeringen). To determine the amount of the wage components, it is calculated how much the employee received in the three months prior to the month in which they became incapacitated for work."
    ],
    [
      "Second Opinion for Disability: When the employee must provide a statement from an expert physician designated by the UWV, as referred to in Article 7:629a BW, the employee has the right to a second opinion from another expert physician."
    ],
    [
      "Transitional Provision for Disability: If the employee became ill or incapacitated for work before 1 January 2004, or was entitled to a WAO benefit on or after 1 January 2004 after a waiting period of four weeks, Article 30 of the CAO Jeugdhulpverlening 2002-2003 also applies to this employee."
    ],
    [
      "Salary Continuation During Disability (First 52 Weeks): If the employee becomes wholly or partially incapacitated for work due to illness, pregnancy, or childbirth, the employee is entitled to 100% of the salary as determined in Article 7.1 for a maximum of 52 weeks, as long as the employment contract continues AND they are incapacitated for work."
    ],
    [
      "Salary Continuation During Disability (Next 52 Weeks): If the employee is still incapacitated for work after 52 weeks, the employee is entitled to salary for another maximum of 52 weeks, but then at 70% of the salary as determined in Article 7.1, unless the following article paragraphs apply."
    ],
    [
      "Salary Continuation During Disability (Second Year at 85%): In the following situations, the employee is entitled to 85% of the salary as determined in Article 7.1 for 52 weeks in the second year of incapacity for work: if the employee performs work that is included in their action plan, aimed at reintegration; if the employee follows schooling or training to return to work; if the employee temporarily performs other work in a lower salary scale, with their employer or elsewhere, unless the employee receives a salary there that is higher than 85% of the salary as determined in Article 7.1."
    ],
    [
      "Salary for Partial Disability: If the employee is partially incapacitated for work, and can therefore perform the agreed work within the meaning of Article 7:629 BW for a number of hours, they receive 100% of their hourly wage for those hours."
    ],
    [
      "Recurrence of Disability: If the employee can work again, but becomes incapacitated for work again within four weeks, no new period of incapacity for work starts, but the periods of incapacity for work are added together. Article 7:629 of the Burgerlijk Wetboek applies here."
    ],
    [
      "Maternity and Childbirth Leave and Salary Continuation: If the employee is on maternity leave and childbirth leave, those weeks do not count towards the salary continuation period from Article 10.4.a and 10.4.b. If the employee receives a benefit due to maternity leave and childbirth leave according to Article 3.7 lid 1 of the Wet Arbeid en Zorg, and this benefit is lower than the salary as determined in Article 7.1, the employer pays the employee the difference."
    ],
    [
      "Income from Other Work During Disability: If the employee is incapacitated for work and earns money during that period, not with their own employer, the employer deducts the amount the employee earns there from the salary continuation as referred to in 10.4.a, b and c. This only applies to work that the employee started after they became incapacitated for work."
    ],
    [
      "Statutory Benefits During Disability: If the employee is incapacitated for work and is also entitled to a statutory benefit during that period, and that benefit is higher than the salary the employer pays the employee based on Article 10.4 members a, b and c, the employee receives that benefit."

      "Employment Contract for Indefinite Period: In Youth Care, work is performed as much as possible by employees with an employment contract for an indefinite period. This ensures quality and continuity in Youth Care and provides employees with more job security. Employers ensure as much as possible the progression of employees with a fixed-term employment contract to an employment contract for an indefinite period with a fixed number of hours per week. To achieve this progression, CAO parties have made the following agreements: an employer gives the employee an employment contract for an indefinite period as much as possible after a fixed-term employment contract; an employer makes agreements with the OR/PVT about the number of fixed-term employment contracts and the number of indefinite-term employment contracts; the employer also makes agreements about fixed-term employees in the organization's training policy; social plans also include agreements for fixed-term employees."
    ],
    [
      "Termination Notice Period: If the employee or the employer wishes to terminate the employment contract, the following agreements apply: The notice period for the employee and the employer is 2 months, unless the notice period must be longer based on Article 7:672 lid 2 BW. In that case, the notice period for the employer is as stipulated in Article 7:672 lid 2 BW. For certain functions, given their nature, the employer can agree on a longer notice period with the employee. This must be stated in the employment contract. This longer notice period may not exceed 6 months and then applies to both the employer and the employee. The notice period starts on the first day of the month following the month in which the employer or employee gave notice. Termination must be in writing. If the employee was 45 years or older on 1 January 1999 and a notice period longer than 2 months applied to them at that time, that longer notice period remains valid as long as the employee works for the employer."

As long as the employee works for the employer, the CAO Jeugdzorg remains applicable. The following articles and chapters of this CAO do not apply to these employees from 1 January 2016: Article 3 - Introduction, Article 3.6 - When does the employment contract end?, Article 3.10 - Arrangements for forced dismissal, Chapter 10 - What if the employee is incapacitated for work? Instead of Article 3.6 and Chapter 10, the text of the law 'Werken na de AOW-gerechtigde leeftijd' applies. Note: this only applies to employment contracts entered into after the employee has reached the AOW-gerechtigde leeftijd. This agreement aligns with what is stated in the law 'Werken na de AOW-gerechtigde leeftijd' regarding fixed-term contracts."

    [
      "Suspension (Non-Active Status): The employer can place the employee on non-active status if they believe that the work within the organization is seriously hindered if the employee continues to work, whatever the cause. The employer may not do this as a disciplinary measure. The employer can place the employee on non-active status for two weeks. They can extend this period once by another two weeks. The employer must investigate during the period in which the employee is on non-active status whether the employee can return to work. If it appears that the employee can return to work, the employer must take the necessary measures to ensure that the employee can resume their work. If the two weeks, or four weeks in case of extension, have passed, the employee has the right to return to work. If the employer has requested a dismissal permit or asked the court to dissolve the employment contract during this period, the employer can decide to keep the employee on non-active status for longer. If the employer decides to place the employee on non-active status, they must inform the employee immediately and confirm this in writing. They must also tell the employee why they are doing this, or in case of extension, why the non-active status is extended. If the employee is placed on non-active status, this has no consequences for their income."

    [
      "Suspension (Schorsen): The employer can suspend the employee if they suspect that there is an urgent reason to dismiss the employee with immediate effect, and if the employer believes that an investigation into the urgent reasons is necessary. What constitutes urgent reasons is described in Articles 7:677 and 7:678 of the Burgerlijk Wetboek. The employer can suspend the employee for a maximum of two weeks. They can extend this period once by a maximum of two weeks. If the employer decides to suspend the employee, or to extend this suspension, they must inform the employee immediately. The employer must state why the employee is being suspended, and how long the employee may not work, or why the suspension is being extended and how long this extension will last. The employer must then confirm this decision in writing as soon as possible. Before the employer suspends the employee, the employer must give the employee the opportunity to tell their side of the story. The employee has the right to seek assistance from a representative in this regard. If the employee is suspended, this has no consequences for their income. If it later appears that the employee was wrongly suspended, the employer must rehabilitate the employee. This means that they officially state that the employee was wrongly suspended. The employer must confirm this in writing. In this case, the employer pays any costs incurred for the representative. The employee can oblige the employer to inform all people to whom they reported the employee's suspension that the suspension was unjustified. If the suspension has proven to be unjustified, but the employer does not want to admit this or has not confirmed it in writing, or if the employer does not want to inform the people who were notified of the suspension about it, this can be a reason for the employee to immediately terminate their employment contract according to Article 7:679 of the Burgerlijk Wetboek."

      "Wachtgeldregeling 1 - Income from New Work: The employee must immediately report to the old employer any income from work or business they will earn or receive from the notification of dismissal. If the employee has income from or in connection with work or business, which they started on or after the day dismissal was announced, this income is deducted from the 'wachtgeld'. This is done as follows: per month, the amount of income the employee has and the amount of the 'wachtgeld' benefit are determined. These amounts are added together. If the result of this sum is more than the calculation basis, the excess is deducted from their 'wachtgeld'. The amount the employee earns from overtime is not considered income in this regard. If the employee had vacation or leave in the period immediately before dismissal, or was placed on non-active status, and the employee has income as referred to in Article 1.6. lid a during this period, then Article 1.6. lid b also applies. If the employee started a business before their dismissal or started another job before their dismissal from which they have income after their dismissal, this is also offset against the 'wachtgeld', as described in Article 1.6 lid b. This rule does not always apply. The 'wachtgeld' is not reduced if the employee can credibly demonstrate that there is no connection between the dismissal and this income or the increase in this income and that they did not start working more hours after their dismissal. The amount the employee earns from overtime is not considered income in this regard. If, due to the nature of the work or income, the employee does not know exactly what their monthly income is and the income must be calculated over a longer period, the employee informs the employer how much they expect to earn over this longer period. A provisional amount is then determined by the employer. That amount is deducted from the 'wachtgeld'. At the end of the period, the employer reviews how much the employee actually earned. The employer offsets the difference with the amount the employee has already received. Article 1.6 members a, b and c and d apply here, whereby offsetting is done over the longer period instead of over each month separately."

      "Wachtgeldregeling 1 - Wachtgeld upon Death: If the employee dies, and the employee is still entitled to 'wachtgeld', their surviving relatives receive an amount. Surviving relatives refer to the persons described in Article 7:674 third paragraph of the Burgerlijk Wetboek. This amount is calculated as follows: the surviving relatives are entitled to an amount equal to the calculation basis. They receive the 'wachtgeld' from the first day after the employee's death for the remainder of the month in which the employee died, and the two full months thereafter. However, if the employee was entitled to a portion of the 'wachtgeld' amount for a reason stated in Article 1.6, 1.7, 1.8 or 1.9, then the surviving relatives receive the 'wachtgeld' from the first day after the employee's death for the remainder of the month in which the employee died, and the two full months thereafter."
    ],
    [
      "Wachtgeldregeling 2 (Appendix 6.2) - Eligibility: Wachtgeldregeling 2 applies to employees mentioned in Article 3.10.a.3 of the CAO Jeugdzorg 2015-2016, if all conditions from Article 2 of this appendix are met. These employees are eligible for a monthly benefit paid by the old employer. The 'wachtgeld' benefit starts on the date of dismissal."
    ],
    [
      "Wachtgeldregeling 2 - Calculation Basis: The calculation basis for the 'wachtgeld' is the salary the employee earned in the month before they were dismissed, including holiday allowance. If the employee does not have a fixed monthly salary, the calculation of the salary in the following situations is as follows: If the employee had a fluctuating salary in the past year, the calculation of the salary is based on the average salary the employee received over the 12 full calendar months before their dismissal, including holiday allowance. If the employee was granted an allowance for irregular services, the average monthly allowance, calculated over the 12 full calendar months before their dismissal, is included in the salary calculation. Here too, the holiday allowance is included. If the salary earned before dismissal is adjusted due to CAO wage increases (other than a periodic salary increase), this adjusted salary is used as the calculation basis, calculated from the date the salary changed."
    ],
    [
      "Wachtgeldregeling 2 - UWV Registration: If the employer has informed the employee that they will be dismissed, the employee must register with the UWV Werkbedrijf within 14 days and ensure they remain registered. However, if the employee is not required to register with the UWV Werkbedrijf according to the rules of the Ministry of Social Affairs and Employment, the employee is exempt from this registration obligation."
    ],
    [
      "Wachtgeldregeling 2 - Service Period: In 'wachtgeldregeling 2', service period refers to: the time the employee had an employment contract for a fixed or indefinite period with their last employer. If the service period has already been included in the calculation of 'wachtgeld' or an unemployment benefit, the months used for the calculation of this benefit or 'wachtgeld' are deducted from the service period, unless the employee is entitled to a longer period of 'wachtgeld', as stated in Article 2.4 lid b."
    ],
    [
      "Wachtgeldregeling 2 - Duration of Wachtgeld: The employee is entitled to three months of 'wachtgeld' for each full year they worked for the employer. The employee is entitled to a maximum of 24 months of 'wachtgeld'. If the employee meets the following three conditions, they are entitled to 'wachtgeld' until the day they reach the AOW-gerechtigde leeftijd: If the employee reaches the AOW-gerechtigde leeftijd within five years at the time of dismissal. If the employee has a minimum of 15 years of service with employers covered by this CAO. If this period has never been interrupted for longer than 1 year."
    ],
    [
      "Wachtgeldregeling 2 - Amount of Wachtgeld: The 'wachtgeld' is equal to the amount needed to supplement (the total of) the following forms of income: 1. the insurance or provision against the consequences of unemployment and/or incapacity for work; 2. income from or in connection with work or business that the employee has received from or after the day of dismissal, in the following manner: Month 1 to 3: up to 93% of the calculation basis. Month 4 to 12: up to 83% of the calculation basis. Month 12 to 24: up to 73% of the calculation basis. If the employee is entitled to 'wachtgeld' for a longer period, based on Article 2.4 lid b, the employee is entitled to a supplement of up to 70% of the calculation basis during this extension period."
    ],
    [
      "Wachtgeldregeling 2 - Income from New Work: The employee must immediately report to the old employer any income from work or business they will earn or already receive from the notification of dismissal. If the employee has income from or in connection with work or business, which they started on or after the day dismissal was announced, this income is supplemented up to a maximum of the percentages of the calculation basis as mentioned in Article 2.5 that apply to the months mentioned in Article 2.5. This is done as follows: per month, the total amount of income the employee has and the amount of the 'wachtgeld' benefit are determined. If the total income is lower than the calculated amount based on the calculation basis as mentioned in Article 2.5 that apply to the months mentioned in Article 2.5, the income is supplemented up to the calculated 'wachtgeld' amount. The amount the employee earns from overtime is not considered income in this regard. If the employee had vacation or leave in the period immediately before dismissal, or was placed on non-active status, and the employee has income as referred to in Article 2.6. lid a during this period, then Article 2.6. lid b also applies. If the employee started a business before their dismissal or started another job before their dismissal from which they have income after their dismissal, this is also offset against the 'wachtgeld', as described in Article 2.6 lid b. This rule does not always apply. The 'wachtgeld' is not reduced if the employee can credibly demonstrate that there is no connection between the dismissal and this income or the increase in this income and that they did not start working more hours after their dismissal. The amount the employee earns from overtime is not considered income in this regard. If, due to the nature of the work or income, the employee does not know exactly what their monthly income is and the income must be calculated over a longer period, the employee informs the employer how much they expect to earn over this longer period. A provisional amount is then determined by the employer. Based on that amount, it is supplemented until the calculated 'wachtgeld' amount per period is reached. At the end of the period, the employer reviews how much the employee actually earned. The employer offsets the difference with the amount the employee has already received. Article 2.6 members a, b and c and d apply here, whereby offsetting is done over the longer period instead of over each month separately."
    ],
    [
      "Wachtgeldregeling 2 - Entitlement to Benefits: If the employee claims benefits under social security legislation, the employee must immediately report this to the old employer. If the employee can claim a WAO benefit or WIA benefit and receives less than 70% of the calculation basis for this benefit, this benefit is deducted from the 'wachtgeld'. The employee then receives the difference. If the employee can claim a benefit under the WW, Ziektewet, WAO or WIA, and they are entitled to this due to their old work, for which this employee is also entitled to 'wachtgeld', then the benefit is deducted from the 'wachtgeld' during the period in which the employee is entitled to this benefit. The employee is then only entitled to 'wachtgeld' if the 'wachtgeld' amount is higher than the benefit."
    ],
    [
      "Wachtgeldregeling 2 - End of Wachtgeld Right: In these situations, the right to 'wachtgeld' ends: If the employee dies. The 'wachtgeld' ends on the day after the employee's death. If the employee is entitled to an AOW benefit. The 'wachtgeld' ends on the day the employee reaches the AOW-gerechtigde leeftijd. If the employee can claim an invalidity pension based on the employment contract on which the 'wachtgeldregeling' is also based. The right to 'wachtgeld' ends on the day the employee claims this pension. If the employee has refused a suitable function offered to them by their former employer from the notification of their dismissal. If the employee behaves in such a way that the employer would have dismissed the employee if they had still been employed."
    ],
    [
      "Wachtgeldregeling 2 - Reduced Wachtgeld: In the following situations, the 'wachtgeld' is reduced if the employee: refuses to accept an offered function, without prejudice to the provisions of Article 2.8; does not make use of the possibility to obtain income in a manner that can be considered suitable for them; has forfeited income as referred to in Article 2.6 due to their own actions or has lost it due to their own fault or actions. The amount by which the 'wachtgeld' is reduced is calculated as follows: Per month, it is determined how much the employee could have earned per month if they had accepted the job or had obtained or retained income, and how high the 'wachtgeld' benefit is. These amounts are added together. If the result of this sum is more than the calculation basis, the excess is deducted from their 'wachtgeld'. The 'wachtgeld' is not paid for the period that the following situations occur: The employee does not comply with the obligations associated with the 'wachtgeld' mentioned in Articles 2.2 and 2.6.a. The employee has a fixed residence abroad, unless their old employer has given them permission for this. The employee does not comply with the agreements with the benefit agency, as a result of which they lose their WW, WAO, Ziektewet, or WIA benefit. The employee does not provide the correct or complete data required to calculate the 'wachtgeld'."
    ],
    [
      "Wachtgeldregeling 2 - Wachtgeld Payment: The employee receives the 'wachtgeld' paid monthly. The employer can pay the 'wachtgeld' in longer installments. This is only possible if the employee gives permission for this."
    ],
    [
      "Wachtgeldregeling 2 - Extra Money for Voluntary Pension Accrual: If the employee is entitled to 'wachtgeld', and chooses a voluntary continuation of participation in the pension scheme they had with the old employer, and continues this for at least half, then the employee is entitled to an allowance for their costs. This allowance is half of the employer's share that the employer would have paid for the employee during a full month if they had still been employed. This is based on the employer's share in the month before the employee was dismissed. If the premium percentage for the pension changed in this month, the employer pays the new percentage."
    ],
    [
      "Salarisgarantieregeling (Appendix 10, 5): The table with guarantee scales applies to the employee who has received a written notification from the employer that the employee has been placed in a guarantee periodic from 1 September 2001. This notification also states (if applicable) the guaranteed salary perspective. This agreement is included in the individual employment contract or in an appendix to the employment contract."

      "Definitions: Deeltijdwerker (part-time worker): employee appointed for less than 1,930 hours annually. Arbeidsgehandicapte werknemer (disabled employee): employee with structural functional limitations. Feitelijk loon (actual wage): CAO wage or a higher agreed wage. Maandloon (monthly wage): weekly wage multiplied by 4.35. Uurloon (hourly wage): actual wage per week divided by 37 hours. Jaarloon (annual wage): monthly wage multiplied by 12. Werkweek (work week): standard 37 hours per week for a full-time employment. Jaarurenmodel (Annual Hours Model): the adopted model incorporating the annual hours norm. Jaarurennorm (Annual Hours Norm): the CAO-described norm of 1,930 working hours annually for a full-time employment. For employees to whom the Annual Hours Model applies, an average work week of 37 hours applies. Vestiging (establishment): agreed location of the company for the application of the employee's terms of employment. Opstappunt (pick-up point): the place from which travel is made together, not being the home or the establishment, to the place where the work is performed. Dienstverband voor onbepaalde tijd (indefinite employment): formerly permanent employment. Dienstverband voor bepaalde tijd (fixed-term employment): formerly temporary employment. Where fixed-term employment is mentioned, it also refers to employment for specific work. Beroeps Praktijk Vormingsovereenkomst (Vocational Practical Training Agreement): formerly apprenticeship agreement. Consignatiedienst (on-call duty): the circumstance that an employee – outside regular working hours – must be available on employer's instruction to perform work as soon as possible upon call. Loon (wage for BTER-loon): all gross wage components related to working hours; fixed annual allowances and payments. Includes: actual wage from current employment; overtime/additional hours/unpleasant hours including inconvenience allowance and shift allowance; 13th month; structural year-end bonus; vacation allowance; paid leave and ADV days, travel hours (not travel expenses); performance bonus on hourly wage; temporary allowance for working in a higher position; temporary allowance for professional knowledge; personal allowances; on-call allowance / availability allowance. Huwelijk (marriage): equated with a lasting cohabitation arrangement evidenced by a notarized cohabitation contract or a registered partnership. Echtgenoot/echtgenote (spouse): equated with unmarried persons of different or same sex, who permanently maintain a common household, unless they are blood relatives in the first or second degree. Inlenersbeloning (borrower's remuneration): the legally applicable remuneration of the employee in the client's service, working in an equal or equivalent position as the temporary worker. Consists of: only the applicable periodic wage in the scale; the applicable working time reduction per week/month/year/period (can be compensated in time and/or money at the temporary employment agency's choice); allowances for overtime, shifted hours, irregularity (including public holiday allowance) and shift allowance; initial wage increase, amount and timing as determined by the client; expense reimbursement (insofar as the temporary employment agency can pay these free of wage tax and premiums: travel expenses, pension costs and other costs necessary due to the performance of the function); periodic increases, amount and timing as determined by the client. Uitzendovereenkomst (temporary employment agreement): the employment agreement where one party as employee is made available by the other party as employer, in the context of the exercise of that employer's profession or business, to a third party, to perform work under the supervision and direction of the third party.       "Occupational health and safety: Employees can undergo a targeted occupational health examination (Stigas or certified company doctor) at employer's expense: once every 3 years for ages 25-44; once every 2 years for ages 45-49; annually for ages 50+. Employer must provide annual medical examination for employees regularly performing spraying with toxic substances. Safety periods for crop protection/pesticides must be observed. Employer must only use specifically trained employees for roadside work. Employer must comply with Arbeidsomstandighedenbesluit regarding shelter and break facilities. Employer promotes EHBO diploma acquisition and maintenance (costs reimbursed). If no toilet at workplace, employee has right to use nearest available toilet."
    ],
    [
      "Branch RI&E: A branch Risk Inventory and Evaluation (RI&E) has been established for the horticulture sector, available via www.stigas.nl. An arbocatalogus (occupational health and safety catalog) is published on www.groenearbo.nl."
    ],
    [
      "Social affairs: Employer discusses social policy with employees annually. Employer must consult employees on ergonomic and safety aspects before purchasing new machinery/tools."
    ],
    [
      "Conscientious objections: Employer respects serious conscientious objections by offering equivalent alternative work (if reasonably possible). Employee must accept. Employee must inform employer in writing, with motivation, of objections and consequences. Employee must limit damage if refusing work. Refusal of incidental work due to serious conscientious objection will not lead to dismissal."
    ],
    [
      "Union contributions and facilities: Union members can deduct union contributions tax-friendly from gross salary (or other wage components) as long as fiscally allowed. Employer provides notice boards for union announcements. Meeting space can be provided for union meetings (after working hours), with 4 working days' notice."
    ],
    [
      "Merger, transfer of control, business termination/reduction: Employer must consult unions early on planned merger, transfer of control, business termination/reduction, and arrange for consequences for union members."
    ],
    [
      "Disputes: A disputes committee provides non-binding advice on CAO interpretation and binding rulings on function valuation disputes. Other disputes are decided by the ordinary court."
    ],
    [
      "Interim changes: If extraordinary circumstances arise, any contracting organization can call for discussion on interim CAO changes. If no agreement, dispute decided by an arbitration committee (binding decision, subject to government approval)."
    ],
    [
      "Dispensation: CAO parties can grant dispensation from CAO application upon request from an employer/group party to another valid CAO, if compelling arguments exist and the other CAO is at least equivalent. Dispensation is for a maximum of the CAO's duration. A dispensation committee handles requests."
    ],

      "Protocols: Structural themes (employment, funds, working conditions, training). Union contact persons (1 for <20 employees, max 3 for >=20 employees), 2-year term, same legal protection as works council members. CAO broadening (new function descriptions added to function handbook, reference functions updated/valued by ORBA). Compliance (social partners focus on informing about CAO compliance). Employment (horticulture sector aims for good employment practices and sustainable labor relations, discourages competition on terms, encourages hiring older workers (45+)). Scope to be reviewed during CAO term. Older employees (social partners inform about subsidy possibilities for hiring older workers).       "All amounts mentioned in this CAO are gross, unless explicitly stated otherwise."

    [
      "Wage scales: Wage scales with periodic increases are associated with the functions listed in the function matrix (Annex XIV). Adult employees receive the CAO wage for their function. Upon employment, adult employees start at 0 periodic increases; 1 or more can be granted based on acquired skills and/or experience. Entry levels A, B, and C are included for special categories of employees, new entrants, and young students. Young students start at entry level A (Article 30). New entrants (not following a BBL program) start at entry level B. Progression through entry levels is A to B to C, followed by periodic 0. BBL students (level 2, 3, or 4) are classified into entry level A (Article 30). Each year on January 1, adult employees receive one periodic increase, provided they have been employed for at least 6 months, until they reach the maximum of their wage scale. The employer can suspend a periodic increase for a maximum of one year due to insufficient performance, provided the employee is notified in writing of the reasons. The employer can grant more periodic increases for very good performance. If a performance-based remuneration system (Annex XVII) is applied at company level, periodic growth through the scale depends on the assessment system. If an employee replaces a higher function for more than one month, they will be paid the difference between their CAO wage and the wage of the observed function (at 0 periodic) at least once a month."
    ],

      "Service anniversary: For a 12.5-year service anniversary, 1/4 gross monthly wage is paid. For a 25-year service anniversary, 1 net monthly wage is paid."
    ],
    [
      "Travel times: Travel from home to the main or branch office or to the designated pick-up point (Article 41, Lid 6) is not reimbursed. For direct travel from home to the worksite and back, 30 minutes per day is considered the employee's own time. Travel time exceeding these 30 minutes counts 100% towards the annual hours norm (up to a total of 9.5 hours per day). If traveling from the employer's location or designated pick-up point, travel time under 9.5 hours per day (including working time and travel time) counts 100% towards the annual hours norm. Hours exceeding 9.5 hours per day (including working time and travel time) do not count towards the norm but are paid out with the applicable allowance. For employees driving a vehicle requiring a C-license on employer's instruction, there is no 'own time'. Loading and unloading personal tools at the company location is not considered actual work and is assumed to take place directly before and after formal working hours. The pick-up point is preferably agreed upon mutually, aiming for the shortest total travel time from home to the pick-up point for all involved employees. The employee is responsible for ensuring clothing, hand tools, etc., are in order before departure, in their own time."
    ],
    [
      "Travel expense reimbursement: Employees receive reimbursement for travel with their own transport from home to the establishment or a designated pick-up point (Article 41, Lid 6) and back. The reimbursement is based on the single travel distance from home to the establishment/pick-up point and is: 0-5 km (round trip): €0.18 per km, maximum €0.86 per day. 5-10 km (round trip): €0.18 per km, maximum €1.04 per day. 10-15 km (round trip): €0.18 per km, maximum €1.29 per day. Voluntary relocation further from the company does not create a right to higher travel expense reimbursement. If an employee travels more than 30 kilometers per day (round trip) from home to the actual worksite (not the main or branch office), they receive €1.29 for the first 30 kilometers and €0.27 per kilometer for additional kilometers. If, in the employer's opinion, the employee must use their own vehicle, or if the employee must use their own vehicle for work during working hours, they receive a reimbursement of €0.27 per kilometer. If work is performed at such a distance from the employee's home that an overnight stay is necessary, a suitable arrangement for a 'night allowance' will be made at company level. The employer will, within the framework of fiscal laws and regulations, monthly assist in the tax-friendly full or partial settlement of any unused space regarding travel expenses for commuting. If monthly processing is not possible, the employer can choose to settle this quarterly, semi-annually, or annually. If fiscal regulations change, this arrangement will be adjusted accordingly."
    ],
    [
      "Work clothing and safety equipment: If no work clothing is provided, an allowance of €2.27 per week is paid for work clothing. Safety shoes and other safety equipment are provided by the employer. These reimbursements and provisions are only for employees who need them for their function. Provided items will be timely supplemented and replaced. Work clothing is provided on loan. The employee must use the provided materials and return them at the end of employment. Private use without employer's permission is prohibited. The employer is obliged to cover the costs of cleaning mandatory safety clothing."
    ],
    [
      "Meal allowance: If an employee performs work (not travel time) after 18:00, the employer will reimburse a warm meal."
    ],

      "Settlement at end of employment: A regular final settlement is prepared. For balances in the annual hours model, Article 22 (annual hours model) and Article 23 (settlement of excess or deficit hours) apply."

      "Wage payment obligations during incapacity for work and right of recourse: The payout percentages are conditional on the employee adhering to company sick leave rules and cooperating sufficiently with reintegration obligations (Wet Verbetering Poortwachter - WVP). Assessment is by an independent expert. The wage for calculation is per Article 7:629 BW (wage the employee would have received if not incapacitated). Employees whose employment ends during incapacity have no right to statutory wage payment or supplements from the day after employment ends. Fully and permanently incapacitated employees entering IVA within the first two years of incapacity are entitled to wage payment supplements. First 26 weeks (within 1st year): 70% of time-based wage paid, supplemented to 100%. Second 26 weeks (within 1st year): 70% of time-based wage paid, supplemented to 90%. Second year of incapacity: 70% of time-based wage paid, supplemented to 75% (or 85% if employee cooperates with WVP reintegration). For employees less than 35% incapacitated (UWV or objectively determined) after the second year, and if employment continues with the same employer, they receive 90% of the time-based wage for a maximum of 5 years. If Ziektewet/WAO/WIA benefit paid via the employer is higher than the employee's applicable wage (after deductions), the employer must pay the excess. Total payment (wage + supplement) cannot exceed the agreed time-based wage. The employer has an independent right of recourse (Article 6:107a BW) if the employee's incapacity is caused by a liable third party. Wage payment for incapacity due to paid work for third parties is governed by Article 12. Vacation rights during incapacity are governed by Article 51. A separate scheme for incapacity exists (SAZAS Sickness Absence Insurance, Annex VIII)."

Vacation pay based on actual gross wage. 22.5 vacation days (180 hours) annually, and 80% of ADV hours (for 58+ years: 23.75 vacation days (190 hours)).       "Performance-based remuneration system (Bijlage XVII): Companies can choose between a basic model (annual periodic increase) or a performance-based model. If performance-based, periodic growth depends on an assessment system. For functions in the function matrix, assessment is based on 6 competencies (3 fixed, 3 variable). For management functions, it's based on 8 competencies (4 fixed, 4 variable). A 4-point scale is used. Salary growth: 0-9 points (0 periodic), 10-15 points (0.5 periodic), 16-21 points (1 periodic), 22-24 points (2 periodics). For management functions: 0-14 points (0 periodic), 15-22 points (0.5 periodic), 23-30 points (1 periodic), 31-32 points (2 periodics). If an employee reaches the maximum of their wage scale and scores 'excellent', they receive a one-time gross payment of €100. A development plan is required for insufficient or moderate performance. The assessment process involves a goal-setting interview, an evaluation interview, and an assessment interview. If the assessment cycle is not properly executed, an employee may object and receive a 'good' rating (1 periodic increase). For new employees, no assessment if employment is less than six months. If an employee changes function and has been in that function for less than six months, the assessment covers both current and previous functions. If longer than six months, only the new function is assessed. If an employee is absent for more than six months due to illness, no assessment is made, and the salary is adjusted based on the last assessment outcome."

    [
      "Pension scheme details: A pension scheme is in effect in accordance with the Statutes and Regulations of the Bedrijfspensioenfonds voor de Landbouw (BPL - Agricultural Sector Pension Fund). Participation is mandatory for employees of an employer affiliated with the pension fund, starting on the first day of the month in which the employee turns 21. The BPL pension scheme is an average wage scheme, where a fixed percentage of the pension base for that year is accrued for each year of service. The pension consists of: Old-age pension (from retirement date until death); Partner pension (payout for the partner if the participant dies); Orphan pension (payout for children up to 24 years if the participant dies); Anw-gap pension (a supplement to the Anw-benefit). The pension date is 67 years, but it is possible to start the pension earlier (from age 60). It is also possible to convert part of the accrued old-age pension into extra partner pension or vice versa. The scheme is executed by TKP Pensioen."
    ],
    [
      "Voluntary early retirement (SUWAS I): A separate collective labor agreement exists for voluntary early retirement for employees in green and agricultural sectors. The scheme is executed by TKP under the responsibility of Stichting uittreding werknemers agrarische sectoren (Suwas-I). Employers and employees pay a levy to Suwas-I. The Suwas scheme is a VUT (early retirement) scheme and is being phased out, only applicable to employees born before January 1, 1950. Conditions apply, including a requirement for the employee to have worked for ten years in the agricultural sector immediately prior to the payout. The Suwas scheme starts between ages 60 and 62, depending on age. The payout is calculated over the SV-wage (wage for Social Security) with a maximum of 1.5 times the maximum SV-wage, normally 80% of the wage, plus a vacation allowance. It is possible to defer VUT. Part-time VUT (50% work, 50% VUT) is possible. Side earnings are allowed under certain conditions. Pension accrual continues during the VUT period, with Suwas covering the full pension premium."

The annual levy for these social funds is calculated based on the wage as defined in Article 2, paragraph 1, sub y. The maximum daily wage is 1.5 times the maximum premium daily wage for employee insurance."

      "Supplementary fund for sickness and incapacity for work (SAZAS): A separate agreement exists for a supplementary fund for sickness and incapacity for work (SAZAS Sickness Absence Insurance). Employers and unions in the agricultural sector offer the SAZAS Sickness Absence Insurance. It includes a Basic package for the employer and a Plus package for the employee. The employer can choose from five variants for the amount of SAZAS reimbursement for their wage payment obligation in the first two years of sickness. Employees automatically participate in the Plus package (unless they opt out), paying 0.75% of their wage (2014 premium level). If an employee is sick for more than half a year and cooperates with reintegration, the Plus package provides additional supplements: 10% in the second half-year of sickness (totaling 100% with employer's payment); 15% in the second year of sickness (totaling 100% with employer's payment); 10% in the third to seventh year of sickness (first five WIA years), calculated over the insured wage. The Plus package also covers the WGA-gap, supplementing the WGA follow-up benefit to a maximum of 70% of the last earned wage (capped at the statutory maximum daily wage) until age 67, assuming an unchanged gap situation. If the employer terminates the insurance, the employee can continue the Plus package individually within two months (0.95% premium); after two months, a health declaration is required. SAZAS also offers an Incapacity Insurance for Agricultural Self-Employed and WIA coverage packages. Absence guidance is also offered."

  "leave_information": [
    [
      "Vacation days: Full-time employees who have been employed for the entire calendar year are entitled to: 148 statutory and 37 extra-statutory vacation hours if they are 18 years or older at the start of the calendar year. 148 statutory and 66.6 extra-statutory vacation hours if they are under 18 years at the start of the calendar year. Unused statutory vacation hours expire 6 months after the last day of the calendar year in which the entitlement arose. Unused extra-statutory vacation hours expire 5 years after the last day of the calendar year in which the entitlement arose. The employer must provide the opportunity to take vacation hours. Vacation hours accrued before January 1, 2012, expire after 5 years. The employer must maintain a proper administration clearly showing the employee's accrued statutory and extra-statutory vacation hours."

The wage for this purpose is the actual wage, excluding overtime (Article 37) and applicable reimbursements. The vacation allowance is paid with the usual wage payment in May, covering the preceding period."
    ],
    [
      "General provisions on vacation days: Employees are prohibited from working for wages or for third parties during vacation days. Employees with at least 10 or 25 consecutive years of service with the same employer are entitled to 7.4 or 14.8 extra paid vacation hours per year, respectively. A consecutive employment period includes situations where an employee with a fixed-term contract works for the same employer, interrupted only by the winter period. The consecutive vacation period is determined by mutual agreement between June 1 and September 1, and must be 15 working days. The employer may, with the consent of the majority of employees, establish a collective company vacation of 15 working days. If deviated, the consecutive vacation must be at least 10 working days. Employees may, in consultation with the employer, take a continuous vacation of up to 7 weeks once every 2 years, provided business circumstances permit and sufficient vacation days have been accrued. If there is a collective company vacation, a minimum balance of 74 hours of freely usable leave must remain on the employee's leave card. If Article 24 is applied, a minimum balance of 10 freely usable leave days must remain for the employee, regardless of the applicable schedule. Other vacation days are taken throughout the year and generally require 6 days' advance notice. The employer may, in consultation with the employees, designate 3 mandatory vacation days (at least one in the winter months), or close the company between Christmas and New Year and designate the intervening days as mandatory vacation. These designations must be made at the beginning of the calendar year or upon employment."
    ],

Employees may take leave during incapacity, subject to company procedures. Work-therapeutic leave, as determined by the company doctor, does not require prior permission but must be reported to the employer and is not deducted from the leave balance."
    ],

      "Calamity leave and short-term absence with pay: Employees are entitled to leave with pay for a short, reasonably calculated period due to very special personal circumstances, including: 1. Death of spouse, live-in parents, live-in parents-in-law or foster parents, and live-in own or foster children: from the day of death up to and including the day of burial or cremation. 2. Two days for the employee's marriage. 3. One day for: childbirth of the spouse (without prejudice to Article 55); employee's engagement; 25-, 40-, 50-, or 60-year wedding anniversary of the employee or (in-law) parents; marriage of own or foster children, (one of) the parents, siblings of the employee or their spouse, provided the wedding ceremony is attended. One day for death and one day for burial or cremation of non-live-in own or foster children, in-law children, grandchildren, (foster)parents, grandparents, in-laws, siblings of the employee or their spouse, provided the burial or cremation is attended. 4. One day for relocation at the employer's request within the same municipality, and two days for relocation at the employer's request to another municipality. 5. The short time necessary to make arrangements in case of a calamity or an acute care task. Necessary medical care (e.g., doctor's visits) should generally take place outside working hours. If this is impossible, the employer must pay the actual wage for the time actually needed for this care. The employee must notify the employer in advance of taking leave, stating the reason, or as soon as possible if advance notification is not possible. The employer may later require the employee to demonstrate entitlement to the leave. If the employee receives a benefit from an insurance or fund related to the employment contract during this leave, the employer may reduce the wage by the amount of the benefit. The employer may reduce the wage by reimbursed expenses saved by the employee due to not performing work."

      "Short-term absence without pay: The employer must grant leave without pay, up to a maximum of 10 days per calendar year, in the following cases, with these days deducted from the annual hours norm: for attending meetings of boards or committees of public law bodies, for which the employee has been designated as a member or substitute member by their union; for attending statutory meetings of their union. The employee must notify the employer in writing of their membership/appointment upon employment. For necessary absence, one week's notice is required (minimum 2 days in special cases). If business interests permit, the employer must grant leave without pay, up to a maximum of 10 days per calendar year, for attending a course provided by a union or a youth organization affiliated with the union, of which the employee is a member. At least 5 days must be taken between November 15 and April 1. The employee must notify the employer as early as possible of their intention to attend such a course. In case of employee relocation, leave without pay is possible: one day for relocation within the same municipality, and two days for relocation to another municipality. The employee is entitled to leave without pay for a short, reasonably calculated period to organize and arrange the burial or cremation of non-live-in parents/in-laws/children/foster children. The employee may also use paid leave for this purpose. This right exists from the day of death up to and including the day of burial or cremation. The employee is entitled to leave without pay for a short period for volunteering, unless business interests oppose it. The employee may also use paid leave for this purpose."

The employee can continue pension accrual during parental leave by paying the necessary pension premium to the employer, who then remits it to the pension fund."

    [
      "Probation period: For indefinite employment contracts, a mutual probation period of a maximum of 2 months can be agreed upon in writing. For fixed-term employment contracts, a probation period of a maximum of two months can be agreed upon if the contract is for more than 6 months and a maximum of 2 years. It is not permitted to agree on a probation period for fixed-term contracts shorter than six months. The written employment contract must state whether a probation period has been agreed upon and its duration (see legal provisions in Annex X)."
    ],
    [
      "End of employment: Employment ends: immediately upon termination by the employer or employee during the probation period (if agreed); or due to an urgent reason as defined in Article 7:678 and 7:679 of the Civil Code (see legal provisions in Annex X); on the day the employee reaches AOW-eligible age; for employees on fixed-term or specific-work contracts, automatically at the predetermined time or upon completion of the specific work (the employer is bound by a notice obligation here, see legal provisions in Annex X); for employees on a Beroeps Praktijk Vormingsovereenkomst, when the training is completed, the training ends at the employee's initiative, or the agreement is terminated under the Wet Educatie Beroepsonderwijs (except for employees who already had an employment contract when entering the BPV agreement); by mutual consent; by dissolution by the subdistrict court; by termination by the employer or employee, where the employer requires permission from the UWV. Fixed-term employment contracts shorter than 6 months end automatically. For employment longer than 6 months, a written notice obligation applies, where the employer indicates whether the agreement will be extended and under what conditions."
    ],
    [
      "Notice periods for indefinite employment: Termination occurs at the end of the month. The notice period to be observed by the employer for an employment contract that on the day of termination: lasted less than 5 years: 1 month; lasted 5 years or longer, but less than 10 years: 2 months; lasted 10 years or longer, but less than 15 years: 3 months; lasted 15 years or longer: 4 months. For employees aged 50 and older, the notice period is a minimum of 3 months. The notice period to be observed by the employee is 1 month. If permission, as referred to in Article 9 of the Buitengewoon Besluit Arbeidsverhoudingen, has been granted, the notice period (Article 11, Lid 3) is shortened by 1 month, provided the remaining notice period is at least 1 month (see legal provisions in Annex X)."

A fixed-term employment contract can only be terminated by the employer before its end date via dissolution by the subdistrict court or by termination after obtaining permission from the UWV. The route to follow depends on the grounds for dismissal. The employee can terminate prematurely with a 1-month notice period. Additionally, employer and employee can terminate the fixed-term employment contract by mutual consent."

From payroll period 10 2013: Employee's wage as if not incapacitated, plus average irregularity allowance from preceding 52 weeks.       "FAQ (from payroll period 10 2013): Payment of minus hours: Yes, minus hours paid in payroll period they arise. If scheduled later, employer does not pay again. If minus hours lead to weekend/overtime work, employee receives special hours/overtime allowance. Employee pays for max 24 hours of idle time per payroll period, max 80 total minus hours. Remaining idle time paid by employer. Part-timer with fixed model generally cannot make up minus hours. If employer schedules minus hours and employee is sick, minus hours deducted from balance. Upon termination, employer can offset minus hours; remaining are at employer's expense. If employer does not use announced shift: if employee has not worked all hours, employer pays all hours (3 worked, rest as minus hours). If max 24/80 minus hours reached, all hours paid. If employee has worked all hours, employer pays 3 hours."

    [
      "ADV day (Definition, Article 1, until payroll period 10 2013): Allocated and paid day off for working time reduction. Without start time of shift after 00:00 and before 24:00. Forward rotating roster: 32 hours without working hours if last shift is night shift; 24 hours without working hours if last shift is other. Backward rotating roster: always 32 hours without working hours. 8 working hours or pro rata for part-time. Definition expires as of payroll period 10 2013."
    ],
    [
      "Working hours (Definition, Article 1): Includes vacation hours, extraordinary leave hours, sick leave hours (incl. waiting day), ADV hours (until payroll period 10 2013). From payroll period 10 2013, includes vacation hours, extraordinary leave hours, sick leave hours (incl. waiting day), minus hours."
    ],
    [
      "Public holidays (Definition, Article 1): New Year's Day, both Easter days, Queen's Day, May 5th (lustrum years), Ascension Day, both Pentecost days, both Christmas days, and government-designated national holidays."

      "Wage supplement employee with an on-call contract (Definition, Article 1, until payroll period 10 2013): Supplement for not accruing vacation hours and not being granted ADV. Definition expires as of payroll period 10 2013."

ADV hours not counted. Definition expires as of payroll period 10 2013."

      "ADV days (Article 21, until payroll period 10 2013): Full-timer: 13 ADV days/year. Part-timer: pro rata. On-call contract: no ADV, instead 5% supplement on basic hourly wage. Employer determines/schedules ADV days. No ADV days in fixed roster for payroll periods 7, 8, 9, 13. Max 3 ADV days per payroll period. Max 5 ADV days per calendar year determined with Thursday weekly schedule (preceding week). Other ADV days determined >= 28 days in advance. Employer can shift ADV day to next payroll period with employee consent. If ADV day cannot be taken due to incapacity/special leave, no compensatory time off."
    ],
    [
      "Roster-free days (Article 22, until payroll period 10 2013): Employee entitled to 8 roster-free days per payroll period (also for part-timers). Employer strives not to schedule on 1st/2nd Christmas Day, New Year's Eve after 16:00, New Year's Day."
    ],
    [
      "Weekly Uninterrupted Rest Period (Article 24, until payroll period 10 2013): At least 36 hours in every 7x24h period, OR at least 60 hours in every 9x24h period. May be shortened to 32 hours once per 5 consecutive weeks. Starts at first work time of day."
    ],
    [
      "Daily Uninterrupted Rest Period (Article 25, until payroll period 10 2013): At least 11 hours in a 24-hour period. May be shortened to 8 hours once per 7x24h period. Starts at first work time of day."
    ],
    [
      "Sunday Work (Article 26, until payroll period 10 2013): At least 16 free Sundays per year. At least 1 free Sunday per payroll period (part of roster-free weekend). At employee's request, 3 free Sundays in continuous vacation (Article 66(4)) designated as roster-free days."
    ],
    [
      "Night Shift (Article 28, until payroll period 10 2013): After night shift ending after 02:00, at least 14 hours uninterrupted rest. May be shortened to 8 hours once per 7x24h period (excl. travel time). After series of consecutive night shifts (min 3, max 7): at least 48 hours uninterrupted rest."
    ],
    [
      "Additional Rules for a Fixed Roster (Article 33, until payroll period 10 2013): Roster-free days included in duty roster: average 8 per 4 weeks. Min 2 periods of 2 consecutive roster-free days, 1 of which is a roster-free weekend. With employee consent, 2 consecutive roster-free days after night shift can be 48 hours without working hours. ADV days included in duty roster. Employer can oblige work on previously determined roster-free day once per payroll period."
    ],
    [
      "Additional Rules for a Fill-in Roster (Article 34, until payroll period 10 2013): 8 roster-free days over 4 consecutive weeks. Min 2 periods of 2 consecutive roster-free days, 1 of which is a roster-free weekend. Employer determines 8 roster-free days >= 28 days before payroll period. Employee can state preference >= 35 days before; if no employer objection/alternative >= 28 days before, employee's preference applies. At least 2 of 8 roster-free days as roster-free weekend, 2 others as fixed (employer cannot oblige work on these 4). Employer can shift/withdraw max 2 of remaining 4 roster-free days.
