    [
      "Scope of agreement",
      "This CAO is a framework CAO. The section up to 'IV Collective Regulations' and all appendices except appendix 1b apply to all companies within the scope. Other articles are in modules and apply to specific branches/sectors. Where employment conditions in a module deviate from the basic section, the module's conditions apply. This CAO applies to employment contracts between employers and employees working in the Fashion, Sport & Lifestyle sector in the Netherlands. An enterprise (or a department, including head office and central warehouse) falls under the sector if at least 50% of its turnover comes from one or more of the following activities: 1. The sale (in a store or via internet) to private individuals or end-users of: a. Fashion articles b. Sport articles c. Shoes d. Paint, paint supplies, and related articles (Detailhandel in Verf en Woninginrichting) e. Garden center articles f. Furniture and home accessories (excluding antique, camping, school, office furniture, and paneling) g. Goods for home furnishing (textile and non-textile), including floor coverings, fixed carpets, runners, window coverings, upholstery fabrics, wallpaper, or other wall coverings h. Kitchens i. Bathroom sanitary and related articles j. Parquet and/or other similar floors, as well as linoleum, cork, plastic, and laminate floors (with specific exclusions for sector 5 or 17 if members of Vereniging van Parketvloerenleveranciers or Parketmeesters) k. Jewelry, gold and silver works, timepieces, gemstones, and related articles l. Products in the field of beauty, human body care, and related articles. 2. The sale of furniture and home furnishing textile goods (by project upholsterers or in a store or via internet) to large consumers or to persons who use the purchased goods in their own business, unless these goods are mainly manufactured in their own business, where the sale of home furnishing textile goods is accompanied by the placement or processing thereof. Large consumers are defined as companies, schools, and institutions in the broadest sense of the word. 3. The performance of technical/craft and service activities related to the sale of articles as under a. Article 1, paragraph 1, sub f (such as installation and assembly) b. Article 1, paragraph 1, sub g (such as laying fixed carpets and runners, applying upholstery fabrics or processing them into wall coverings, repairing floor coverings or wall coverings) c. Article 1, paragraph 1, sub h (such as installation or connection), except when the activities are performed in the contracting business in civil and utility construction or in DIY retail or building materials retail d. Article 1, paragraph 1, sub i (such as installation or connection), except when the activities are performed in the contracting business in civil and utility construction or in DIY retail or building materials retail e. Article 1, paragraph 1, sub j (such as laying these floors). 4. The laying of floors (not linked to their sale) mentioned under Article 1, paragraph 1, sub j. 5. The commercial application of wallpaper, except when this is done in the contracting business in civil and utility construction or in the painting and finishing business. 6. The manufacturing and processing of curtains in curtain ateliers, provided they are members of INretail."

      "General Binding Declaration (AVV)",
      "Most provisions of this CAO are applicable to all employers and employees falling under the scope of the CAO upon its entry into force or during the term of the AVV. Some provisions are excluded from the AVV request and are therefore only binding for organized employers and their employees. The Ministry of Social Affairs and Employment (SZW) may also exclude provisions from the AVV. CAO provisions that are by their nature not eligible for AVV include, for example, provisions on pensions, reinsurance of employers' own risks, and provisions not related to labor. The AVV decision, published on www.cao.szw.nl, specifies which provisions fall within the AVV. The AVV enters into force on the day after the date of issue of the Staatscourant in which the AVV decision is placed, or on the date specified in the decision, and runs until the end date of the CAO at the latest."

  "wage_information": [
    [
      "Job Classification System",
      "This CAO uses a job evaluation system (FUWAM) with 9 function levels (A to I) and corresponding wage scales. Each function level contains full-fledged functions, so each scale can be an end scale. Employees are classified at the correct function level using the Handboek Functie-indeling voor Fashion, Sport & Lifestyle or online tools like www.functiewijzer.nu. Appendix 1c contains a summary of the level sheets. Disputes over job classification can only be settled based on the detailed Handboek Functie-indeling. Employers wishing to apply their own wage structure can request dispensation from the Sociale Commissie."
    ],
    [
      "Guaranteed Wage Determination",
      "The wage paid to an employee is linked to a function group in which the employee's function is classified. The employee receives a wage that is at least equal to the amount determined for their age or function years according to the wage scales (Appendix 1b). The amounts listed are gross and are stated both on an hourly basis and per month for an average working time of 38 hours per week (for Wonen, 37 hours per week)."
    ],
    [
      "Hourly Wage Calculation",
      "For calculating the hourly wage, the monthly wage is divided by 164.67, and the weekly wage by 38. For Wonen, the monthly wage is divided by 160.33, and the weekly wage by 37."
    ],
    [
      "Wage Adjustment (General)",
      "Per July 1, 2016, the wages of employees who have not yet reached the new maximum of their scale are increased by 0.83%. The scale wages are increased by this percentage as of this date. During the term of the CAO, on January 1, 2017, July 1, 2017, and January 1, 2018, the scale wages and the wages of employees who have not yet reached the new maximum of their scale are increased by the percentage by which the Statutory Minimum Wage is increased. If the percentage set by the government on one of the aforementioned dates is lower than 0.4%, then the aforementioned wages and wage scales are increased by 0.4%. Employees who do not receive a wage increase per January 1, 2017 because they are above scale, receive a one-time payment of 0.5% over the total wage in 2016 per January 1, 2017. The total wage is the wage over which holiday allowance is calculated."
    ],
    [
      "Wage Adjustment (Mode, Verf en Woninginrichting)",
      "To restore differentiation in the wage structure Mode and Verf en Woninginrichting, wage scale A is abolished, and wage scale B becomes the new scale for functions in A and B."
    ],
    [
      "Wage Adjustment (Sport en Schoenen)",
      "Differentiation is also restored in the wage structure for Sport en Schoenen. The differences that existed upon the introduction of the new wage structure on January 1, 2012, are reintroduced into the wage structure. This means that wage scales B to I as they were on January 1, 2012, are increased by the percentage that restores these differences per July 1, 2016. When an employee reaches an experience year on or after July 1, 2016, they are classified at the step in the scale with the next higher amount. Scale A remains the scale that applies on July 1, 2016."
    ],
    [
      "Performance-based Pay",
      "The employer determines whether performance-based pay is applied. If a works council is present, the employer must consult with it. If it is decided to apply performance-based pay, the appraisal system applies to all employees aged 22 and older. The system must meet established criteria (see Appendix 1d). Examples of simple to more complex appraisal systems are offered for the sector. Performance-based pay is possible after a one-year appraisal cycle has taken place. Employees younger than 22 years are not performance-based, but only receive their age increase."
    ],
    [
      "Wage by Age",
      "Wage increases due to age take effect on the first day of the payment period in which the employee has their birthday. This applies to employees who join before their 22nd birthday. This also remains the date on which an experience year is granted after their 22nd birthday."
    ],
    [
      "Experience Year",
      "For employees aged 22 or older at the time of employment: Wage increases due to reaching an experience year take effect on the first day of the payment period in which the employee has one year of experience in the function. If a performance-based pay system is applied within a company, the experience year is replaced by the appraisal increase. A company can choose to grant the appraisal increase at a fixed time of the year – this fixed time then applies to all employees – which means that the increase date does not have to coincide with the employee reaching their experience year. In that case, every employee with at least 6 months of experience in a function must be eligible for the appraisal increase."
    ],
    [
      "Experience Wage for 21-year-olds",
      "Employees who, at the moment they reach the age of 21, have at least two years of experience in their function at the company, receive the scale wage of a 22-year-old. The provision regarding experience wage for 21-year-olds does not apply to employees working in home furnishing retail (woondetailhandel)."
    ],
    [
      "Placement in Another Function",
      "If the employee is placed in another function, they are classified in the function group belonging to that function as of the date of appointment to that function. From that day, they receive the corresponding wage, unless a functional probation period has been agreed upon. When placed in a higher function group, the employee is entitled to at least the same wage."
    ],
    [
      "Wage for Public Holiday",
      "The employee is entitled to continued payment of wage on a public holiday (for definition, see Article 2 of this CAO) if they would normally perform work on that day. In the case of an irregular work pattern, this can be determined if the employee has performed work on the day in question in at least 8 of the past 13 weeks. In determining this, all realized hours (see Article 2) count as worked days. If the 8 out of 13 criterion is met, the wage is paid based on the average number of hours worked on those days. If the employee performs work on the public holiday, the allowance scheme of Article 7.4 also applies."
    ],
    [
      "Module for Shoe Retail: Diploma Allowance",
      "If an employee obtains one of the following diplomas/certificates (or an equivalent past qualification), they receive a monthly allowance on top of their guaranteed wage. For 'Gezond lopen adviseur' (Advisor for Healthy Walking, Cursus Adviseur Pasvormschoenen), the allowance is €33.70. For 'Gezond Lopen Specialist' (Specialist for Healthy Walking, Landelijk erkend diploma Schoenconsulent), the allowance is €46.70. Employees are obliged to follow any offered follow-up training for these qualifications to retain the allowance. Part-time employees receive the allowance proportionally. In case of multiple eligible allowances, only the highest one applies. An employee is not entitled to this allowance if their full-time wage plus the gross allowance exceeds their guaranteed wage. This allowance is in addition to any other allowances from Article 7 of this CAO and applies regardless of the number of hours worked."
    ],
    [
      "Module for Garden Centers: Job Classification and Wage Table",
      "Before December 1, 2016, employers classified their employees' functions in FUWAM. Functions in catering, animal, and service desk not yet in FUWAM will be described before October 1, 2016. On December 31, 2016, employees with their current wage are classified into the Loontabel Tuincentra Nieuw. The wage scale is equal to the function level determined by classification. This classification only affects the employee's wage when they reach an experience year. If the employee turns 23 or is older, they fictively move to the next step in the Loontabel Tuincentra bestaande medewerkers and then to the next higher step in the Loontabel Tuincentra Nieuw. If the employee is not yet 23, they move to the step in the Loontabel Tuincentra Nieuw corresponding to their age. If the wage development perspective in the Loontabel Tuincentra Nieuw is worse for the employee than if the Loontabel Tuincentra bestaande medewerkers had remained applicable, they have a right to a guarantee of the old perspective for five years. Annually, the employee's table wage (as described above) is compared with the wage they would have had if the Loontabel Tuincentra bestaande medewerkers had remained applicable, with a 0.4% wage increase per January 1, 2017, and all wage increases of the CAO Fashion, Sport & Lifestyle from July 1, 2017. If this old wage is higher, that higher wage becomes their new wage. After the 5-year compensation period (December 31, 2021), the employee's wage will not decrease. Youth wages up to 22 years in the Loontabel Tuincentra Nieuw will be 5% higher than the corresponding youth wages in the Loontabel Mode in CAO Fashion, Sport & Lifestyle. If the government adjusts legislation or regulations in the future regarding the equalization of youth wages for 22-year-olds and younger to the Statutory Minimum Wage for 23-year-olds, the 5% difference for 22-year-olds will lapse."
    ],
    [
      "Module for Garden Centers: Wage Development",
      "Until July 1, 2017, the wage development for employees in garden centers is separate from CAO Fashion, Sport & Lifestyle. Per September 1, 2016, the wages of all employees in garden centers are increased by 1%. The scale wages for Tuincentra bestaande medewerkers are increased by the same percentage as of this date. Additionally, employees who are employed on December 31, 2016, receive a one-time payment in December of 0.5% over the total wage of 2016. The total wage is the wage over which holiday allowance is calculated. On January 1, 2017, the scale wages and the wages of employees who have not yet reached the new maximum of their scale are increased by 0.4%. Employees who reach their experience year on January 1, 2017, first receive the applicable step increase and then, if eligible, the 0.4% wage increase. From July 1, 2017, the wage development for garden centers will be linked to the wage development of Fashion, Sport & Lifestyle. If the economic situation between the two branches demonstrably differs significantly, parties may decide to make deviating wage agreements."
    ],
    [
      "Module for Home Furnishing Retail: Wage Development and Wage Table",
      "From April 1, 2017, a new wage structure with a new system is applicable (Appendix 1b). From January 1, 2017, to April 1, 2017, the old Wonen wage structure (also in Appendix 1b) still applies. Until July 1, 2018, wage development is separate from CAO Fashion, Sport & Lifestyle. Per April 1, 2017, wages are increased by 2.75%. Employees are classified into the new wage structure with their increased salary, meaning the wage will almost always fall between two steps. Upon reaching an experience year, the step to the next higher step is made if the employee is entitled to a step increase. Collectively granted wage increases in 2015 and 2016 can be deducted from the 2.75% wage increase. Per January 1, 2018, wages are increased by 1%. Both mentioned wage increases apply to the portion of the wage up to the maximum of the scale in which the employee's function is classified. After the term of this CAO, the method of wage increase will align with what is agreed in CAO Fashion, Sport & Lifestyle."
    ],

      "Module for Jewelers and Perfumeries: Job Classification and Wage Structure",
      "Before August 1, 2017, employers classified their employees' functions in FUWAM. From this date, the Mode wage structure of CAO Fashion, Sport & Lifestyle applies to new employees. Current employees are classified into the new wage structure with their wage increased on July 1 or August 1, 2017 (including any increase to compensate for the abolished shopping evening allowance, and if the Sunday allowance reduction compensation was monetary). This means the wage will almost always fall between two steps. Upon reaching an experience year, the step to the next higher step is made if the employee is entitled to a step increase."

Collectively granted wage increases from January 1, 2016, can be deducted from the wage increase per August 1, 2017. Per January 1, 2018, employees receive a wage increase equal to the percentage by which the statutory minimum wages increase, with a minimum of 0.4%. Employees who earn more than the maximum of their scale only receive an increase on the portion of their salary up to this maximum."

      "Pension Scheme Details",
      "The employer is obliged to be affiliated with the Bedrijfstakpensioenfonds Detailhandel (Industry Pension Fund Retail) based on a mandatory declaration from the Minister of Social Affairs and Employment, unless exempted or granted dispensation. Conditions may be attached to the exemption. The Stichting Bedrijfstakpensioenfonds Detailhandel is responsible for the execution of the pension scheme. The board of the foundation has entrusted the management and administration of the pension scheme to TKP in Groningen. This entity also handles the collection of pension fund premiums."

  "leave_information": [
    [
      "Vacation Year",
      "The vacation year coincides with the calendar year, unless other agreements have been made with the works council. The employee accrues vacation rights over this period."
    ],
    [
      "Vacation Hours with Continued Pay",
      "The employee is entitled to vacation with continued pay for 182.4 hours per year (based on an average 38-hour workweek or an average 7.6-hour workday). For Wonen, this is 185 hours (based on an average 37-hour workweek or an average 7.4-hour workday). Vacation rights are deemed to accrue proportionally over the year. An employee who is employed for only part of the vacation year is entitled to a proportional part of the vacation mentioned in this article."
    ],
    [
      "Accrual of Vacation Hours",
      "For the accrual of vacation hours, the number of agreed contract hours (or basic hours) is generally used as a basis. If the employee works more than their basic hours during a vacation year, vacation hours are also accrued over the excess, unless the extra hours are taken as time off in accordance with Article 4."
    ],
    [
      "Determination of Vacation Hours to be Taken",
      "When determining the number of hours to be taken for vacation (day), the number of hours the employee would work according to their schedule is generally used as a basis. If no schedule has been established, the number of vacation hours to be taken in the relevant week is equal to the average number of hours the employee worked per week in the last 13 weeks preceding the vacation. If that reference period is not representative, the last 52 weeks preceding the day of vacation are used."
    ],
    [
      "Consecutive Vacation",
      "The employee is entitled to two consecutive weeks of vacation per calendar year."
    ],
    [
      "Vacation During Sickness",
      "If the employee has received permission to go on vacation during the sickness period (see also the absence regulations in Appendix 2 of this CAO), these days will be deducted from the vacation balance."
    ],
    [
      "Expiration of Statutory Vacation Days",
      "Statutory vacation days expire 6 months after the calendar year in which they were acquired, but the employer may, in consultation with the works council, deviate from this expiration period and then apply the old expiration period of 5 years. The agreed arrangement then applies to all employees."
    ],
    [
      "Collective Vacation Days",
      "The employer may, in consultation with the works council, designate a maximum of 2 days per year as mandatory collective vacation days for the company. Deviations per branch and location are possible."
    ],
    [
      "Extra Vacation Hours Due to Age",
      "The employee aged 50 to 55 years is entitled to extra vacation hours equal to 1/5 of the number of basic hours per week. The employee aged 55 to 60 years is entitled to extra vacation hours equal to 2/5 of the number of basic hours per week. The employee aged 60 years or older is entitled to extra vacation hours equal to 3/5 of the number of basic hours per week, unless there is an adjustment of working hours in accordance with Article 15. In that case, these extra vacation hours lapse. The employee accrues extra vacation hours per vacation year."
    ],
    [
      "Extra Vacation Hours Due to Length of Service",
      "The employee with a length of service of 25 to 40 years is entitled to extra vacation hours equal to 2/5 of the number of basic hours per week. The employee with a length of service of 40 years or more is entitled to extra vacation hours equal to 4/5 of the number of basic hours per week. The employee accrues extra vacation hours per vacation year."
    ],
    [
      "Overlap of Extra Vacation Hours",
      "If the extra vacation hours scheme due to age overlaps with the extra vacation hours scheme due to length of service, only the scheme that is most favorable to the employee applies."
    ],
    [
      "Retention of Wage for Extra Vacation Hours",
      "For the extra vacation hours, the employee retains the right to their normal wage."
    ],
    [
      "Holiday Allowance Year",
      "The holiday allowance year runs from June 1 to May 31. The employer may, in consultation with the works council, agree on a different holiday allowance year."
    ],
    [
      "Holiday Allowance Amount",
      "An employee who has been employed for the entire holiday allowance year receives a holiday allowance of 8% of the actual gross wage earned in the holiday allowance year, paid no later than June, provided the bandwidth specified in Article 16 WML is met."
    ],
    [
      "Special Leave (General)",
      "Special leave enables an employee to attend special events, particularly in their private life. For a part-timer, the right to leave applies proportionally to the hours they would normally work on the day(s) concerned. In addition to the statutory leave regulations from the Wet Arbeid & Zorg, the employee is entitled to special leave with continued pay in the following cases:"
    ],
    [
      "Special Leave for Marriage",
      "For their own marriage: on the day of the marriage and the day before or after. For attending the marriage of one of their children (including step- and foster children), siblings, as well as one of the parents-in-law: the day of the marriage. For their 25th, 40th, 50th, and 60th wedding anniversary and that of their parents and parents-in-law: the day of the anniversary or the day on which the celebration takes place."
    ],
    [
      "Special Leave for Death",
      "For the death of the spouse or own cohabiting children: from the day of death up to and including the day of the funeral. For the death of one of the parents, parents-in-law, own non-cohabiting children, foster or stepchildren: the day on which the funeral takes place and the day of death or a day between the death and the funeral. For attending the funeral of grandparents, partner's grandparents, grandchildren, siblings, brothers-in-law, and sisters-in-law: the day of the funeral."
    ],
    [
      "Special Leave for Service Anniversary",
      "For the employee's 25th, 40th, and 50th service anniversary: 1 day."
    ],
    [
      "Special Leave for Relocation",
      "For relocation: the day of relocation (once per year)."
    ],
    [
      "Special Leave for Job Application",
      "If the employment is terminated by the employer: for a reasonable time to apply for a new job and to verbally explain the application."
    ],
    [
      "Special Leave for Medical Reasons",
      "The starting point is that the employee makes appointments in their own time. If that is not possible, there is a right to special leave for a mutually agreed reasonable time for: undergoing a mandatory medical examination; and necessary visits by the employee to the doctor, dentist, or specialist."
    ],
    [
      "Equating Sustainable Cohabitation to Marriage",
      "An employee who has entered into a legally registered partnership or a sustainable cohabitation arrangement is equated with a married employee."
    ],
    [
      "Standard Sickness Absence Regulations (Appendix 2)",
      "Sickness absence refers to the absence of employees from the workplace due to unsuitability for performing (adapted) work due to illness or accident, based on a medical cause. Employees must report sick by phone before the start of working hours, but no later than 9:00 AM, to their employer or designated contact person. If sick later in the day or during working hours, report as soon as possible. Timely reporting is crucial for continued wage payment or sickness benefits. Information to provide includes: start date of sickness, whether a doctor has been consulted, expected recovery date, current address and phone number, and whether any work can still be performed. Employees are not required to disclose health complaints or the link between illness and working conditions to the employer, but must provide this to the ARBO service/company doctor. Changes to the nursing address must be reported immediately. Employees must be available for control visits by the employer and ARBO service/company doctor at home or at the nursing address during working hours. For the first six weeks, employees must be home until 10:00 AM and from 12:00 PM to 2:30 PM. After 6 weeks, new agreements on availability are made in the plan of action. Employees must cooperate with recovery and follow medical advice. If partially fit for work, employees must accept suitable alternative work offered by the employer, or seek it outside the company if not available internally. For long-term absence (by the 6th week), the ARBO service conducts a problem analysis, and a plan of action is developed by the 8th week, outlining re-integration goals and measures. An absence file is maintained confidentially by the employer, accessible to designated personnel, and employees have the right to inspect it and request corrections. Medical information is kept in a separate file by the ARBO service. Upon recovery, employees must report to the employer immediately. Between the 46th and 52nd week of sickness, an evaluation takes place for the re-integration report and WIA application. Employees can request a second opinion from UWV if they disagree with the ARBO service or employer's actions. Non-compliance with regulations can lead to sanctions, including partial or full cessation of wage payment or dismissal in serious cases."
    ],
    [
      "Module for Garden Centers: Vacation Days",
      "A full-time employee receives 25 vacation days each year."
    ],
    [
      "Module for Home Furnishing Retail: Vacation Days",
      "A full-time employee receives 25 vacation days per vacation year. That is 185 hours, based on a 37-hour workweek, or an average 7.4-hour workday.   "leave_information": [
    [
      "Vacation days (Part A)",
      "Vacation year runs from January 1 to January 1. Employees 18 years and older: at least 25 days per year (minimum 3 consecutive weeks, rest as leave days). Legal entitlement: 4 times the agreed weekly working hours. Employees younger than 18: 28 days per year. Extra-legal vacation days can be saved. Pro-rata for partial year (1/12 per month, 15+ days count as full month). Employer can change vacation period for weighty reasons, must compensate demonstrable damage. Hindu employees can take a vacation day on Diwali. Islamic employees can take a vacation day at the end of Ramadan and on Eid Curban."
    ],
    [
      "Expiration of vacation days (Part A)",
      "Legal vacation days expire six months after the last day of the vacation year in which they were accrued. Extra-legal vacation days expire five years after the last day of the vacation year in which they were accrued. Unused legal vacation days (due to incapacity or operational reasons) can be used for training days or funding (maximum 10 days), if work-related and agreed upon. If legal vacation days are not taken by May 31 following the end of the vacation year, the employer sets the dates."
    ],
    [
      "Holiday allowance (Part A)",
      "8% of the wage or sickness benefit earned between June 1 and May 31 of the current year. Excludes incidental allowances, gratuities, and benefits in kind. Includes fixed shift allowance (Article 44) and irregular hours allowance (Article 39). Paid in May."
    ],
    [
      "Schedule-free days (Part A)",
      "13 schedule-free days per year with retention of wages. For 5-shift workers, Article 16 paragraph 2 applies. Minimum 6 days scheduled by November of the prior year. 4 days reserved for flexible working hours (Article 17A). Maximum 3 consecutive schedule-free days. Employer can designate 3 collective schedule-free days per location annually. If incapacitated on a scheduled day, the day is not lost; a replacement day is agreed upon after recovery. Days not taken due to incapacity or operational reasons expire after 5 years. No schedule-free days are granted after 6 months of incapacity (with short interruptions counting as continuous)."

    [
      "Special leave with pay (Part A)",
      "Unless coinciding with a usual non-working day: Death of spouse/life partner/child: day of death until day of burial/cremation, minimum 4 days. Death of parent/parent-in-law: 2 days. Burial/cremation of blood/affinity relatives (straight line up to grandparents, not spouse/parents/children), brother/sister/brother-in-law/sister-in-law: 1 day. Employee's marriage/registered partnership: 2 days. Marriage/registered partnership of child/foster child/grandchild, brother/sister/brother-in-law/sister-in-law, father/father-in-law, mother/mother-in-law (if attending ceremony): 1 day. 25th wedding anniversary (employee, parents, parents-in-law, if 1 year continuous service): 1 day. 40th, 50th, 60th wedding anniversary (parents, parents-in-law, grandparents, if 1 year continuous service): 1 day. 40th wedding anniversary (employee): 1 day. First Holy Communion, baptism, Berith mila, confession, Bar mitzvah of child/foster child/grandchild (if attending ceremony): 1 day. Attending national meetings of CAO union parties as official delegate, or training courses by unions (if work allows, timely consultation): maximum 12 days per year. (For union officials/CAO committee members, no 'within working hours' condition, always right to free day with pay up to 12 days per year). Employee relocation: maximum 1 day per year. Fulfilling legal/government obligations without financial compensation (e.g., appearing before company committee, if cannot be done outside working hours, no government compensation): maximum 1 day. Short absence (doctor/dentist): as much as possible outside working hours. If not possible, reasonable time allowed. Specialist visit: reasonable time with pay. If special leave coincides with public holiday or vacation (for specific events like death of spouse/parents/children or employee's marriage), employee gets extra time off equal to duration of leave. Loss of pay if employer not notified in time."

    [
      "Vacation days (Part B)",
      "Vacation year runs from June 1 to May 31 (employer can use January 1 to December 31 for vacation days/hours). Legal vacation hours: 4 times the agreed weekly working hours (0.0769 hours per hour entitled to wage). Extra-legal vacation: 5 days per year (0.0192 hours per hour entitled to wage). Total accrual: 0.096 hours per hour worked (where entitled to wage/compensation). No accrual over hours compensated with holiday allowance or overtime in money. Civil Code provisions apply for accrual, expiration, and prescription of vacation rights (including during incapacity). Apprentice vacation hours: Accrued over school hours (unpaid, part of contract). Duration of vacation day: Average weekly working hours / average weekly working days (over full contract duration, maximum last 52 weeks). Right to 3 consecutive weeks of vacation. Can be limited to 2 weeks (employee request or business necessity), then another 1 consecutive week. Employee and employer agree on vacation times. Employer can designate dates for vacation hours exceeding extra-legal days. Employer can change agreed consecutive vacation period for weighty reasons, must compensate demonstrable damage."

      "Unless coinciding with a usual non-working day: Death of partner/child: 4 days (minimum day of death to burial/cremation). Death of parent/parent-in-law: 2 days. Employee's marriage/registered partnership, or child's/sibling's/parent's: 1 day. 25th, 40th, 50th, 60th wedding anniversary (employee, children, parents, grandparents): 1 day. Union meetings/training: maximum 12 days per year (official delegate, work allows, timely consultation). Union officials/CAO committee members always get free day with pay (maximum 12 days per year). Leave taken on day of event, consecutive if multiple days."
    ],
    [
      "Schedule-free days (Inflightcatering, Part A.C)",
      "Employer can schedule schedule-free time (Article 22 paragraph 1) in hours instead of days. Employees can buy maximum 22 schedule-free days per year, which must be taken in the same year. If employer prevents taking, days are reimbursed. Article 56 paragraph 3, 4, 5 apply."
    ],
    [
      "Additional weekend arrangement (Inflightcatering, Part A.C)",
      "A 'free weekend' is a free Sunday, preceded by a free Saturday or followed by a free Monday. If the roster has fewer than 22 free weekends annually, compensation days are granted: 0 free weekends = 21 compensation days, 20 free weekends = 2 compensation days, 19 free weekends = 3 compensation days, 18 free weekends = 4 compensation days, 17 free weekends = 5 compensation days (maximum). Maximum 5 weekends can be scheduled as free days by the employer after consultation. If fewer than 22 free weekends annually, financial compensation up to 50 hourly wages: 1st less = 6 hourly wages, 2nd less = 8 hourly wages, 3rd less = 10 hourly wages, 4th less = 12 hourly wages, 5th less = 14 hourly wages. No compensation day if no work due to incapacity, special/unpaid leave during scheduled weekends. Compensation days paid quarterly. Financial compensation paid in January of the next year."
    ],
    [
      "Spaarurenregeling (Saving hours scheme) (Education Catering, Part A.D)",
      "For locations with fixed company closure, if vacation days are less than non-working days due to closure, employer and employee can agree on a saving hours scheme. Employer informs employee about closure periods and the difference in hours to be saved. Recognized holidays are included. Employer provides a monthly overview of saved hours. Accrual continues during incapacity. If sick during school holiday/saving hours, hours are deducted. Overtime/additional work paid in the next wage period. Saved hours paid out upon termination. Employee authorizes employer to save hours by not paying out a portion of worked hours each wage period. If this scheme is used, Article 17A (flexible working hours) does not apply."

Overtime/additional work paid in the next wage period. Saved hours paid out upon termination. Employee authorizes employer to save hours by not paying out a portion of worked hours each wage period."

      "Up to 6 months employment: no probation period. Longer than 6 months up to 2 years: 1 month probation period. From 2 years or longer: 2 months probation period. Indefinite period: 2 months probation period. During and upon termination of the probation period, both employer and employee can terminate the employment contract without observing a notice period. This also applies if the employee is incapacitated for work during the probation period."
    ],
    [
      "Chain of employment contracts (Part A)",
      "If, between the same parties, within a maximum period of three years, more than three fixed-term employment contracts have succeeded each other with intervals of no more than six months, the last employment contract is considered to have been entered into for an indefinite period. When the employee has performed the same work immediately prior to the employment through a temporary agency relationship, this period, regardless of the number of temporary agency contracts, up to a maximum of three years, does not count in the chain of three years and 3 contracts."
    ],
    [
      "Notice periods for fixed-term contracts (Part A)",
      "For contracts of six months or longer, the employer must inform the employee in writing at least one month before the contract ends by operation of law whether it will be continued or not. For contracts shorter than six months, if either party does not wish to continue, they must notify the other party in writing at least one week before the contract expires. Failure to give timely notice results in compensation equal to a maximum of one month's salary."

For such a termination, the new employer will follow the appropriate procedure via UWV, unless the contract can be terminated in another legally valid way."

If an employee with an indefinite contract becomes an apprentice, the employment contract remains in effect even if the practical training agreement expires or is terminated."
